Two isolated PowerShell 5.1 runs passed **8/8** checks and generated byte-identical artifacts: JSON SHA256 `6893DB2355A2FA2BA0E21F95FCBE589D5C5BDEC2D5E1680311C81E7F04663772`; CSV SHA256 `7827D4E2EA7274960806D124DD4CC9F681564DAE53EF8723D53D9B099C2992E7`.
- Twelve rows compare 12:12/ridge X, 12:12/ridge Y and 10:12/ridge X against M78 U-01..U-04. The relation is `z_eave - z_finished_floor >= T + b_eave`, with `T` inherited from M103 and all datum/build-down values unassigned. The most demanding conditional geometric threshold is -3.166667 ft for 12:12/X (U-03), -1.805556 ft for 10:12/X (U-03), and 5 ft for 12:12/Y (U-01/U-04). These are not evaluated pass/fail results; generic bands, gross rectangular proxy areas, room classification and code applicability remain conditional. A variable ceiling profile needs actual geometry before calculating compliant-area fractions.
- The highest-value missing section inputs are finished-floor to geometric-eave elevation difference and the roof/eave-to-finished-ceiling build-up. 38 1/3 x 40 ft remains hypothetical; U-02 is geometry-only; bathrooms and fixture zones are excluded; M-06/B remains unassigned. No selected-D, RVT, IFC or RFA changed. No code-compliance, structural-safety, certification or construction-readiness claim follows.

## M-06 commercial-duty toilet comparison refreshed (2026-10-28)

- At the owner's request, refreshed the KOHLER Highcliff Ultra K-96057 comparison against the manufacturer's current US/Canada sheet dated 2026-10-27. It is explicitly a floor-mount top-spud **flushometer bowl**. The sheet's diagram gives a 26-1/8 × 14-5/8 in body, allows 10- or 12-in rough-in, and lists a 2-1/8-in trapway. It offers separate manual flushometer options K-76320 at 1.1 gpf, K-76321 at 1.28 gpf, and K-76322 at 1.6 gpf.
- The fixture supply requirement is at least **25 psi flowing pressure at 25 gpm**. Manufacturer language that the bowl handles low-pressure/flow buildings does not cancel that specific minimum. The latest sheet's nominal rim height is 16-5/8 in; KOHLER's product page says 16-7/8 in. Its older 2024 sheet lists 35–80 psi static pressure, but the newer sheet omits that range, so it remains unconfirmed. The flushometer must match the bowl's rated flush volume for the manufacturer's full-system efficiency/performance claim.
- Trade-off: the bowl is compact in plan for a commercial flushometer model, but the high instantaneous supply demand, separate valve and required service coordination could dominate a small house half-bath design. No domestic supply data, confirmed installed height, exact Revit family, M-06 layout, product selection, or plumbing approval exists. The earlier Champion PRO 610AA001.020 remains the owner-selected **first residential investigation**; Highcliff is only the requested commercial comparison. No purchase or room assignment occurred.
- Updated `Research/M06_Toilet_Options_2026-10-27.md` with official current and prior KOHLER sources, the dimensional discrepancy, valve options and no-claims. No geometry or source model was changed.

## Evolution host-probe orientation gate hardening (2026-10-28)

The cited API page is Revit API version 26.4 documentation; it supports the call pattern, not the unrun Revit 2027 host result.

## pyRevit/Python.NET Reassessment (2026-10-28 05:11 EDT)

- Updated the existing tooling assessment using primary Autodesk, pyRevit and Python.NET documentation. Autodesk confirms Revit 2027 uses .NET 10 and recommends SDK 10.0.100; pyRevit's current project docs map Revit 2027+ to `net10.0-windows`, and v7.0 announces its agent runtime. The release page's explicit Revit 2027 compatibility entry is under v6.5.0, so it is not attributed to v7.0.
- Reconciled the important caveat: pyRevit v7.0's release notes advertise the agent host/MCP server, while the current agent-runtime docs still label it experimental and state in-Revit validation on Revit 2024/2025 only. The release therefore supports considering a controlled Revit 2027 test; it does not establish agent-runtime validation on 2027.
- Documented Python.NET's distinct role as a CLR bridge, not a Revit API host; pyRevit's CPython interpreter is process-wide and module state survives pyRevit reloads until Revit exits. The agent runtime's `ask` approval and transaction safeguards are useful, but `readonly` does not sandbox machine-side effects. Model data is sent to the configured provider and run artifacts are kept as plaintext locally for up to 14 days.
- Recommendation remains PowerShell/JSON + C# RevitPilot for deterministic generation and model edits; Python stays offline for IFC validation. No pyRevit, MCP server, software, add-in registration or model transmission was installed or enabled. Any pilot requires explicit owner approval and a sanitized disposable file.

## M91 - Make the M83 Revit host study tolerant of template levels - 2026-10-30

## M102 - Connecticut sloped-ceiling code research - 2026-10-30

- Rechecked Connecticut DAS primary sources on 2026-10-30. The agency continues to list the 2022 CSBC based on 2021 I-codes as current; its adoption page says the anticipated 2026 codes' July 1, 2026 effective date is delayed pending Legislative Regulation Review Committee approval.
- The official 2022 CSBC amended IRC §R305.1 gives baseline ceilings of 7 ft in habitable spaces/halls and 6 ft 8 in in bathrooms/toilet/laundry rooms. Its sloped-room exception uses minimum 5 ft height across required room floor area and 7 ft across at least 50% of required area. The bathroom/toilet fixture exception requires usable fixture height; shower/tub showerhead requires 6 ft 8 in across at least 30 × 30 in at the showerhead.
- M101's roof plane is measured relative to an unassigned eave, not a finished ceiling above a finished floor. Floor/eave relationship, eave/wall location, roof and ceiling build-down, room classification and fixture position are unknown; no M101 case is called compliant or noncompliant.
- Added citations and caveats to `Research/Connecticut_Roof_Code_Research_2026-10-28.md`, and criteria/status to `PLAN.md`. This is a statewide preliminary basis only; municipality and future permit date remain unknown.

## M112 - Reconcile split-level stair arithmetic with Connecticut's amended riser limit - 2026-10-30

- Checked the official Connecticut DAS 2022 CSBC PDF and regulations page. Amended IRC R311.7.5.1 sets 8.25 in maximum riser with 3/8 in maximum variation within a flight; amended R311.7.5.2 sets 9 in minimum tread and 3/8 in variation. Town, project classification and permit path remain unknown, so this is a preliminary statewide basis only.
- Read source levels from selected-D study: -5/0/+5 ft. Each adjacent transition is 60 in. The inherited comparison uses 8 equal 7.5 in risers, 7 x 11 in treads (77 in straight run); the separate owner half-story spec prefers 6 x 7.25 in (43.5 in) and a 5-7 riser range. Seven equal risers for 60 in would be 8.5714 in and exceed the cited CT maximum; eight at 7.5 in are the minimum count under this arithmetic. This clarifies rather than selects the D comparison.
- Corrected the generic 7.75 in code-limit wording in `Architecture/Stairs_Half_Story.md`; updated CO-021 to make the actual section-design choice explicit. No datum or stair geometry changed, and no native Revit stairs or IFC elements were authored.
- Added `Tools/analyze_m112_stair_rise_code_basis.ps1` and the report package. Two independent Windows PowerShell 5.1 replays pass **11/11** and produce matching report hashes: JSON `32D818FDEBA289EBF423EED7377F0B5A90EC772ECA679286A743EA68D2AF01AF`; checks `AE00F1DEDDBF8C912410BB8C467782CF798B0882E6C18E2B7CC8E3FBCD982D50`. The report is source-bound to the study, decision, owner stair-intent, CT research note and analyzer script, and refuses an external output path.
- `Architecture/M112_Stair_Rise_Code_Basis_2026-10-30/README.md` gives exact commands and assumptions. CO-021 remains open; actual structural floor heights, assemblies, openings, landings, headroom, guards, egress, support and code applicability remain unknown. No construction or compliance claim.

## M116 - Conditional Connecticut EERO wall-zone screen - 2026-10-30

Future municipality, site, permit date and code path remain unknown.
- Recorded the conditional sleeping-room basis: Connecticut amended IRC R310.1 requires an operable emergency escape/rescue opening per sleeping room, discharging directly to a public way or qualifying yard/court; amended R310.2.1 gives a 5.7 sf net-clear-area minimum and grade-floor/below-grade exception; adopted 2021 IRC R310.2.2 sets 20 in clear width and 24 in clear height; amended R310.2.3 limits the bottom of clear opening to 44 in above floor. The grade exception is not assumed.

After correcting the orientation summary, it passes **21/21** checks twice with byte-identical JSON/check outputs. The NE group contains 44 north-long blocks (13.6667 x 9.1667 ft gross; 164/110 in north/east contact) and 20 east-long blocks (9.1667 x 13.6667 ft; 110/164 in). 20 x 24 in is only 3.33 sf; area math at minimum width needs 41.04 in clear height, or 34.2 in width at minimum height.
- This identifies a promising perimeter zone only. There is no actual exterior wall, window product, net opening, sill, grade, qualifying yard/court, public-way route, roof/eave or structure. - The analyzer computes minimum and area-median ideal plane rise for U-01 through U-04 across both M158 room-block arrangements and all six 8:12/10:12/12:12 ridge-X/ridge-Y cases. The median is solved by deterministic bisection over the room rectangle's area distribution and verified to split each interval equally. **48 rows** pass **16/16** source and arithmetic checks.
- Two Windows PowerShell 5.1 runs (tracked output and `.local/revalidation/M162-repeat/`) reproduce JSON SHA256 `6C8087DAC2AA0297953BB4AA8D6D87F2DF22FA06F4E46348EC31DF6AF0D549A2` and CSV SHA256 `CF487F1FB539E6A0AB605F58F07E4C3F8B47761669C7DC278B89FC5F0749D903` byte-for-byte.

A steeper plane raises interior regions but does not remove this boundary constraint. The conditional height relation is `z_eave - z_finished_floor >= max(5 - minimumRise, 7 - medianRise) + b_eave`; all variables remain unassigned and no result is evaluated as passing or failing.
- The dated authority basis is the official 2022 CSBC amended 2021 IRC R305.1 text. The full block is only a required-floor-area proxy; municipality, permit cycle, formal code classification, actual legal required area, floor/eave datum, ceiling build-down, walls and roof assembly remain unresolved. This is not code compliance. No selected-D, Revit/IFC, incoming RFA, roof, room block, or remote state changed. See `Architecture/M162_M158_CT_Sloped_Ceiling_Thresholds_2026-10-31/README.md`.

The manufacturer lists the counter stool at 23.75 in seat height and its cafe stool at 28.75 in; the project target is 24–26 in. The counter option is therefore 1/4 in below the stated band and remains a comparison candidate, not a selection.
- The public bundle and both RFA members are preserved in ignored `.local/revit-pilot/family-intake/candidates/m174-mien-tauri-stool/`. The bundle SHA256 is `79DB0B184E79A62FDCDB4528DA6D335BD1D7094BDCFD51737E6B0DACAA884F85`; counter family `TAURI-STL-24-N-N.rfa` is `720F3F1D172285E62C6B4EC0ECEE3A31421DA34B8926AD4D80B30C9B9572F53D`; cafe family `TAURI-STL-29-N-N.rfa` is `100C27E657149C0F0D22D6EA037772E4920EF0CB5FDF1FB6277B19AD3A902186`. Revit's earlier read-only Family Editor view showed Furniture / `Shell_Red` / `Height = 23.75 in`; the source was closed without saving.
- Added `CandidateFamilyGeometryCommand.cs` to load one hash-pinned quarantined family into a fresh Imperial project, measure each active type's positive-volume symbol solids, record bounds/parameters, save a disposable RVT, and report that no instance was placed and selected-D was not opened. Bounds use Revit `Solid.GetBoundingBox()` and transform all eight corners through the box and nested-instance transforms, avoiding face-tessellation vertices as purported exact extrema. Autodesk documents that a solid box is stored in local coordinates and its `BoundingBoxXYZ.Transform` must be applied; `Transform.Multiply` composes the child transform before the parent. Sources: [Solid.GetBoundingBox](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2026/ENU/Revit-API-MainReference/files/html/716fecd9-7d09-fa61-5c02-de714259a214.htm), [BoundingBoxXYZ.Transform](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2026/ENU/Revit-API-MainReference/files/html/297887ab-69bb-548e-cfb6-a3a23f410604.htm), [Transform.Multiply](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2026/ENU/Revit-API-MainReference/files/html/d1ff39c4-3abd-f69c-d73d-c008b38f2d8c.htm). Path validation confines sources to quarantine and outputs to the ignored family-intake derivative directory; an existing non-empty output directory is refused. Failed inspections recheck the source fingerprint.
- Added `validate_candidate_family_geometry.ps1`. PowerShell parser validation reports zero syntax errors. A synthetic positive fixture passes **11/11** checks; a negative fixture with a stale expected source SHA256 exits nonzero as intended. These are validator self-tests only, not Revit family geometry evidence.
- Re-ran the repository's documented offline RevitPilot suite with `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest discover -s Tools\RevitPilot\tests -v`: **49/49 tests pass**. No dependency was installed. This suite covers existing contracts and IFC validators; it does not substitute for executing the new command inside Revit.
